c:T4b92c,<h2 id="what-an-ope-is">What an OPE is</h2>
<p>Think of a local operator as a <em>disturbance</em> stamped onto the worldsheet at a point. When you bring two disturbances close together, from far away they look like one combined disturbance — some superposition of the operators the theory has to offer. The <strong>operator product expansion (OPE)</strong> is the precise Taylor-like series for that merger:
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">\mathcal O_i(z)\,\mathcal O_j(w)=\sum_k C^k_{ij}(z-w)\,\mathcal O_k(w).\tag{1}</span>
It is not an approximation. Inside a correlation function (with all other operators kept far away) it is an <em>exact</em> identity, convergent up to the next nearest insertion. The singular terms — the ones that blow up as <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z\to w</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span></span></span></span> — carry all the physics; they play the role that commutators play in canonical quantization, telling you how operators transform and what algebra they close into. Everything finite we bundle into "<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>…</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+\ldots</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="minner">…</span></span></span></span>".</p>
<p>For a free boson the <em>entire</em> theory is one contraction. Learn the double pole below, and every OPE is bookkeeping — count the ways two <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>'s can touch, and Taylor-expand.</p>
<p>The working recipe:</p>
<ol>
<li>Write composites with normal-ordering colons <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>:</mo><mtext> </mtext><mo>:</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">:\ :</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace"> </span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span></span></span></span> (the quantum "don't self-contract" instruction).</li>
<li>Contract every pair of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>'s across the two operators, each contraction <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>→</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\to</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.3669em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span></span></span></span> one propagator.</li>
<li>Keep only singular powers of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z-w</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li>Taylor-expand any surviving field from <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span> to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li>Read off weights by matching to the stress-tensor template.</li>
</ol>
<p>This is just Wick's theorem, reread as a short-distance calculus.</p>
<h2 id="the-one-number-the-propagator">The one number: the propagator</h2>
<p>The Euclidean free-scalar action and its equation of motion are

The logarithm is a warning: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> itself has no clean transformation law under conformal maps, so <strong>the fundamental field <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> is not a good CFT operator.</strong> Its derivative is. <span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">\boxed{\,\partial X(z)\,\partial X(w)=-\frac{\alpha'}{2}\frac{1}{(z-w)^2}+\text{non-singular}\,}\tag{2}</span>
This single double pole is the whole dynamical content of the free boson. The <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha'</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7519em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7519em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> out front will reappear as <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\hbar</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6889em;"></span><span class="mord">ℏ</span></span></span></span> would — it marks these as quantum effects.</p>
<p>For the string's <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> embedding coordinates, attach a spacetime index and the flat metric:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><msup><mi>X</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>∼</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X^\mu(z)\,\partial X^\nu(w)\sim-\frac{\alpha'}{2}\frac{\eta^{\mu\nu}}{(z-w)^2}.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" The <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> (mostly-plus signature) means contractions can carry a timelike minus sign — yet each of the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> directions contributes an <em>identical, positive</em> amount to the central charge below, because there the two indices meet as <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta_{\mu\nu}=D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span>. There is no sign subtlety in the OPE route to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span>; the well-known "timelike sign squares away" caveat belongs to the mode-commutator route in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/22-polyakov-action-and-cft/virasoro-algebra-and-central-charge/" class="wiki-link">Virasoro algebra and central charge</a>, not here.</p>
<h2 id="the-weight-dictionary">The weight dictionary</h2>
<p>An operator <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="script">O</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal O</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">O</span></span></span></span> has weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>h</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(h,\bar h)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0812em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span></span><span style="top:-3.2634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> if under <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>→</mo><msup><mi>z</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z\to z'(z)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" style="height:1.0019em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">h</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1944em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7519em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9449em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord accent mtight"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">h</span></span><span style="top:-2.9634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord mtight">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">O</span></span></span></span>. Equivalently — and this is the version we use — its singular OPE with the <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/22-polyakov-action-and-cft/worldsheet-stress-tensor/" class="wiki-link">stress tensor</a> reads
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000">T(z)\,\mathcal O(w)=\frac{h\,\mathcal O(w)}{(z-w)^2}+\frac{\partial\mathcal O(w)}{z-w}+\ldots\tag{3}</span>
The double pole hands you <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span></span></span></span>; the single pole is always <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi mathvariant="script">O</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial\mathcal O</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">O</span></span></span></span> (the operator's own derivative). An operator is <strong>primary</strong> when its <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="script">O</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T\mathcal O</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mord mathcal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">O</span></span></span></span> OPE has nothing worse than that double pole — no higher poles. The combination <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>h</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta=h+\bar h</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord">Δ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8312em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span></span><span style="top:-3.2634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is the scaling dimension, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>h</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">s=h-\bar h</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">s</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8312em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span></span><span style="top:-3.2634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> the spin. All that follows is: build <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span>, contract, and match to (3).</p>
<p>Throughout, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo <h2 id="example-1-tpartial-x--partial-x-is-a-weight-1-primary">Example 1: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T\,\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> — <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> is a weight-1 primary</h2>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> holds two <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>'s; either can contract with the lone <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>, so a combinatorial factor 2 appears:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo Matching to (3): the double-pole coefficient is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1\cdot\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">⋅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h=1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>; the single pole is exactly <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial(\partial X)=\partial^2X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> as required — nothing worse. So <strong><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span> is a primary of weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1,0)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span></strong>, the prototype holomorphic current. <p>The normal-ordered exponential <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow></msup><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">:\!e^{ikX}\!:</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">ik</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span></span></span></span> is the <strong>tachyon vertex operator</strong> — the local operator that creates the string's ground state. The weight is <em>proportional to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation look dimensionless). This is why the marginality condition <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mover aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" tachyon mass shell <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" <h2 id="example-3-tt--the-central-charge-c1">Example 3: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T\,T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> — the central charge <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span></h2>
<p>Now the stress tensor with itself. Each <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> carries two <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>'s, so there are two contraction patterns. Contracting <strong>both</strong> pairs (2 ways) makes the leading quartic pole; contracting <strong>one</strong> pair (4 ways) makes the term proportional to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span>:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>T</mi><mo <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math notation="box"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0404em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6954em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">c</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.34em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:1em;"></span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">(one free boson).</span></span></span></span></span>
That quartic pole <em>is</em> the <strong>central charge</strong>. Physically it is the failure of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> to be a genuine tensor — the quantum anomaly in how the stress tensor responds to conformal maps, and equivalently the theory's count of degrees of freedom. One free boson carries <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> decoupled bosons give <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> (the quartic poles simply add, all with the positive sign noted above). This is the OPE face of the object analyzed in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/22-polyakov-action-and-cft/virasoro-algebra-and-central-charge/" class="wiki-link">Virasoro algebra and central charge</a>, and the reason the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> embedding coordinates push <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> toward the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mtext>total</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{\text{total}}=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord text mtight"><span class="mord mtight">total</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> condition that fixes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>.</p>
<h2 id="weight-ne-primary-a-caution">Weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo mathvariant="normal">≠</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\ne</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mrel"><span class="mrel"><span class="mord vbox"><span class="thinbox"><span class="rlap"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="inner"><span class="mord"><span class="mrel"></span></span></span><span class="fix"></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace nobreak"></span><span class="mrel">=</span></span></span></span></span> primary (a caution)</h2>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation the Example-1 result once more produces a <em>cubic</em> pole,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo <h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>State-operator map.</strong> The line "<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>h</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h+\bar h=2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7778em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8312em;"></span><span class="mord accent"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8312em;"><span style="top:-3em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span></span><span style="top:-3.2634em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.25em;"><span class="mord">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> marginal <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>⇒</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Rightarrow</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.3669em;"></span><span class="mrel">⇒</span></span></span></span> tachyon mass shell" is orientation only; the careful dictionary between vertex-operator weights and the string spectrum lives in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/23-ghosts-vertex-operators-and-d26/state-operator-correspondence/" class="wiki-link">State-operator correspondence</a> and <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/23-ghosts-vertex-operators-and-d26/vertex-operators-and-physical-state-conditions/" class="wiki-link">Vertex operators and physical-state conditions</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Normal ordering.</strong> Colons are kept here wherever a coincident product could confuse; every same-point product of fields inside <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> is normal-ordered by definition.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>

<details class="callout callout-success">

mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. Classically there is no propagator to contract against — fields at a point just multiply — so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>k</mi><mi>X</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e^{ikX}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">ik</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> would be dimensionless, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>. The nonzero weight is entirely the short-distance quantum contraction; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>α</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha'</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7519em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7519em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">′</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> marks the spot where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ℏ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\hbar</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6889em;"></span><span class="mord">ℏ</span></span></span></span> entered.</p>
